## Document 006: 10月美国就业数据点评：就业市场反弹，紧缩预期延续

Source PDF: all report pdf/10月美国就业数据点评：就业市场反弹，紧缩预期延续.pdf
Report source: 爱建证券
Date: 2026-11-09
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

证券研究报告
总量研究 / 宏观研究 2026 年 11 月 09 日
细分研究
就业市场反弹，紧缩预期延续
——10 月美国就业数据点评
相关研究
-
投资要点：
 事件：11 月 6 日美国劳工部公布就业数据显示，10 月美国新增非农就业 16.5 万人，失业率
为 4.1%，较上月改善。
 核心观点：10 月美国就业市场明显反弹，新增岗位大幅增加，失业率小幅回落，劳动力市场
韧性有所增强。（1）总体看，新增非农就业明显回升，失业率小幅下降，就业市场短期有所
修复。（2）结构看，服务业为新增就业主要支撑，耐用品制造业表现较好，但部分传统服务
行业就业偏弱，行业分化仍存。（3）工资看，薪资同比增速小幅放缓，环比增速有所回升，
整体工资上涨压力相对温和。（4）政策预期看，就业市场反弹降低了美联储对劳动力市场走
弱的担忧，叠加通胀仍处高位，美联储加息必要性仍存，但油价回落或推动紧缩节奏放缓。
 总体：新增就业明显反弹，失业压力有所缓解。
 美国新增非农就业明显回升，失业率小幅下降，劳动力市场扩张动能有所恢复。（1）新增非

农就业大幅增加。10 月美国新增非农就业 16.5 万人，较上月明显回升，显示此前偏弱的就
业增长有所修复，企业招聘需求有所改善。同时，新增就业规模仍低于 8 月水平，劳动力市
场尚未恢复至此前较强的扩张状态。（2）失业率小幅回落，就业市场压力有所缓解。10 月失
业率由上月的 4.2%下降至 4.1%，新增失业人数由增加转为减少，反映劳动力市场压力边际
缓解。（3）私营部门为就业增长的主要支撑。10 月私人部门新增就业明显增加，贡献了大部
分新增非农就业，说明企业部门招聘需求有所恢复。总体看，美国新增就业回升，失业率下
降，就业市场明显改善，劳动力短期扩张动能有所恢复。
 结构：服务业支撑增强，制造业就业边际改善。
 私营服务业是新增就业的主要支撑，制造业就业有所回暖，但行业表现仍分化。（1）制造业
就业有所改善，耐用品制造业表现有韧性，是制造业就业增长的主要支撑，非耐用品制造业就
业则有所减少。（2）专业和商业服务、贸易运输等行业支撑服务业就业增长，就业保持较强
增势；信息业就业延续减少，金融活动就业小幅增加。（3）部分传统服务行业就业偏弱，教

育、住宿和餐饮以及艺术和娱乐等行业就业均有所减少。总体看，服务业就业增长主要由专业
和商业服务、贸易运输等行业带动，传统服务行业表现相对疲弱，制造业就业虽有所改善，但
整体扩张仍不均衡。
 工资：薪资同比增速小幅放缓，环比上涨压力仍存。
 美国私营部门平均时薪同比小幅回落，但环比增速有所回升，薪资上涨压力总体温和。（1）
薪资同比增速继续放缓。10 月美国私营非农部门平均时薪同比增长 3.1%，较上月小幅下降，
反映工资中长期上涨压力有所缓解。（2）薪资环比增速有所回升。10 月平均时薪环比上涨
0.4%，较上月有所提高，显示短期工资上涨仍具一定韧性。总体看，薪资同比增速放缓，但
环比涨幅回升，工资对服务价格和核心通胀的支撑作用仍需关注。
 政策：就业韧性支撑进一步加息，油价回落或放缓紧缩节奏。
 就业市场反弹叠加通胀高位，美联储进一步加息必要性仍存，但油价回落将缓解通胀压力，后
续加息节奏或趋于放缓。（1）就业市场改善，为美联储货币政策提供紧缩空间。新增非农就
业回升、失业率下降，反映美国劳动力市场仍具韧性，为维持偏紧货币政策提供了条件。（2）

通胀仍处高位，但油价回落有望缓解通胀压力。美国中期选举后，市场预期特朗普政府可能寻
求与伊朗达成协议，国际原油价格明显回落。若油价维持低位，能源价格对整体通胀的推升作
用将减弱。（3）进一步加息概率较高，但节奏趋于放缓。就业市场韧性与通胀压力支持进一
步加息，但油价回落或降低通胀上行风险，美联储后续加息或更加审慎，紧缩节奏趋缓。
 总结而言，10 月美国就业市场明显反弹，新增非农就业回升，失业率小幅下降，劳动力市场
韧性有所增强。结构上，服务业和耐用品制造业支撑就业增长，行业分化仍然明显；薪资同
比增长放缓，环比增速回升，整体薪资压力相对温和。往后看，就业韧性与通胀粘性仍支持
美联储维持紧缩立场，但国际油价回落有望缓解能源通胀压力，后续加息节奏或趋于放缓。
 风险提示：美国就业数据超预期，美国通胀水平超预期，地缘冲突风险。
证券分析师
陈国文

评级说明
投资建议的评级标准
报告中投资建议所涉及的评级分为股票评级和行业评级（另有说明的除外）。评级标准为报告发布日后 6 个月内的相对市场表现，也即：
以报告发布日后的 6 个月内的公司股价（或行业指数）相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数的涨跌幅作为基准。其中：A 股市场：沪深 300
指数（000300.SH）；新三板市场：三板成指（899001.CSI）（针对协议转让标的）或三板做市指数（899002.CSI）（针对做市转让标的）；
北交所市场：北证 50 指数（899050. BJ）；香港市场：恒生指数（HIS.HI）；美国市场：标普 500 指数（SPX.GI）或纳斯达克指数（IXIC.GI）。
股票评级
买入 相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅大于 15%
增持 相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在 5%～15%之间
持有 相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在-5%～5%之间

卖出 相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅小于-5%
行业评级
强于大市 相对表现优于同期相关证券市场代表性指数
中性 相对表现与同期相关证券市场代表性指数持平
弱于大市 相对表现弱于同期相关证券市场代表性指数

分析方法可能存在局限性，请谨慎参考。

世界経済：AI・防衛関連需要を受け景況感は好調。一方でインフレ圧力の広がりも確認
 9月のグローバル製造業PMIは53.0（8月52.3）と堅調。非製造業は54.5（8月53.6）と40か月ぶりの高水準
― 製造業はAI・防衛関連投資需要を受け生産、新規受注ともに上昇。非製造業も幅広い業種で業況が改善
― 一方、仕入・販売価格指数はともに上昇。エネルギーや輸送価格上昇によるインフレ圧力の広がりを示唆
 国・地域別では米国・ユーロ圏が製造業・非製造業ともに改善。とりわけ米国の好調が目立つ
― 米国ではAI関連支出の拡大に伴い機械・設備投資および生産が増加。非製造業においても情報通信業が突出して好調
― ユーロ圏でも、AI投資に支えられたIT関連サービスが特に力強く拡大。専門・商業サービスも改善

### Page 3

金融市場概況：投資家心理が悪化し、日米とも株安・金利低下
 AIインフラ投資への信用不安の高まりから、日米株価はハイテク株を中心に下落
― 米新興クラウドサービスの社債価格急落が株安の契機に。原油高に伴うインフレ懸念も下押し圧力に
― 投資家心理が悪化するなかで、安全資産とされる日米国債に資金が流入、前月までの金利上昇基調は一巡
 ドル円は、1ドル＝150円台後半で方向感の乏しい展開
― 日米金利とも低下し、日米金利差は概ね横ばい圏で推移
― 原油高が輸入増を通じて円安要因となる一方、日米株安に伴うリスクオフや仏情勢悪化に伴うユーロ安は円高要因に

米国：インフレ率は高止まり、油価再加速で当面は物価高が続く懸念
 原油高や関税の影響から物価は加速
―9月のコアCPIは前月比＋0.3％（年率＋4.1％）と8月（前月比＋0.3％、年率＋3.5％）から伸びが拡大
― コアサービスでは住居費や輸送サービスなどが押し上げに寄与。コア財については、衣料品や玩具などが上昇。関税引
き上げに伴う価格転嫁が続いている模様
 足元で原油価格が再加速していることを踏まえれば、当面は物価の上振れリスクが残存する懸念
― シカゴ連銀が公表する週次の小売物価指標は9月後半から10月初めにかけて上昇しており、物価の高止まりを示唆
3
（出所） 米労働省より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

欧州：G7は原油・ディーゼルの協調放出で合意。米国の輸出制限リスクは後退
9

― 背景にディーゼル価格高騰。中間選挙を控えたトランプ大統領は、禁輸も辞さない姿勢を強調（米国は世界需要の4.9％

欧州：ディーゼル需給逼迫は残存。欧州のインフレ上振れ要因に
10
 ロシアのディーゼル輸出は足元で急減。中東からの輸出減、中国の輸出停止も重なり、世界需要の約6％が失われた格好
―G7の1億バレル放出で当面の供給不安は緩和するものの、構造的な供給不足の解消には至らず
―IEAも、中東からの原油輸出は回復する一方、石油製品の供給制約はなお深刻と指摘。ディーゼル需給の逼迫が続け
ば、輸送・物流コストを通じたインフレ圧力が残存
 欧州では9月HICPが再加速。ディーゼル高が輸送・物流コストを通じて追加的なインフレ上振れ要因となるリスク
―9月消費者物価（HICP）は前年比＋3.8％（前月：同＋3.2％）と伸び加速。エネルギー価格の上昇が主因
今般の備蓄放出量と停止中のディーゼル輸出

欧州：EU・中国はハイブリッド車の輸入制限で暫定合意。同様の措置は他の品目に広がる可能性
11
 EUと中国は、貿易不均衡の是正措置について暫定合意。中国からのハイブリッド車輸入は従来見通しの半分以下に
―EUが中国産EVに追加関税を賦課した2024年以降、追加関税の対象とならないハイブリッド車輸入が急増
― 欧州委員会の試算では、今回の措置によって2030年までの4年間で数百万台の輸入を削減見込み。直近では、EUが輸
入するハイブリッド車の約半分を中国車が占めており、域内自動車市場に一定のインパクト
 欧州委員会は、幅広い産業で貿易不均衡を是正する必要性を示唆。同様の措置が他の品目にも広がる可能性
― 欧州委員会のモニタリング対象（輸入増加と価格下落が併存する品目）は、中国からの輸入品が最多

欧州：長期金利上昇を受け、一部ECB高官は追加利上げの必要性低下を指摘
13
 フランスでは国内政治・財政面の不安から長期金利が上昇。イタリア・ギリシャなど周辺の高債務国にも波及
― フランス国債の対独利回り差は急拡大し、一時欧州債務危機（2012年）以来の水準に。その後、大統領選の有力候補と
目されるルペン氏（国民連合）が財政赤字を縮小する意欲を示し、拡大はひとまず一服
―ECBによる介入（国債買い入れ等）の可能性については、複数の高官が検討段階にないとの見方を示す
 長期金利の上昇は需要を抑制。ECB高官からは追加利上げの必要性が低下するとの見方も
― エネルギー高によるインフレ圧力と金利上昇による景気下押し圧力が併存。ECBの政策判断は一段と難しくなる可能性
仏長期金利上昇の経済影響（左）と直近のECB高官発言（右）

中国：7～9月期の実質GDPは＋4.5％。インフラ投資の加速で、通年の成長率目標を確保へ
 7～9月期の実質GDP成長率は前年比＋4.5％。1～9月の累計で同＋4.6％と、通年目標「＋4.5～5.0％」の範囲内
―7～9月期は、純輸出が＋1.8％Ptと成長をけん引する一方、総資本形成は＋0.6％Ptまで縮小。内需の弱さ鮮明
― 中国政府は地方政府専項債の発行枠を追加（2,500億元）。インフラ投資を加速させ、成長率目標の達成を確保へ
 9月の小売は同＋1.1％と、前月（同＋0.4％）から加速。メモリー高騰が通信機器の売上額（同＋31.1％）を押し上げ
― 投資は同▲7.2％（前月：同▲10.6％） 。前年の反動増は限定的で、インフラ投資は同▲10.5％と2ケタ減が続く

― 生産は同＋5.5％（前月：同＋5.2％）。生産量では、産業用ロボット（同＋18.9％）や集積回路（同＋18.4％）が堅調

中国：政府は住宅ローンの利子補給で市場テコ入れ。人民元相場は緩やかな元高が継続
 中国政府が9/28、新規住宅ローン金利の年率1％分を補助する政策を発表。低迷が続く不動産市場をテコ入れ
― 対象物件の購入金額を150万元（3,500万円）以下に限定し、低中所得者層の住宅ローン負担を軽減する
― これにより、余剰在庫に苦しむ地方都市での実需を掘り起こし、市況のさらなる悪化に歯止めをかけたい考え
 人民元相場は、中国の貿易黒字が過去最高水準を記録する中、緩やかに元高が進行し1ドル＝6.6元台に突入
― 高水準の貿易黒字を背景とした人民元買いの実需が強いことから、当面は緩やかな元高が続く見込み
― ただ、米FRBの利上げで米中金利差が拡大しており、さらなる拡大が意識されれば元安に転じる可能性も

日本：旺盛なAI関連需要が製造業の景況感を押し上げ。利益計画は大幅上方修正
16
 大企業の業況判断は製造業が6四半期連続の改善。非製造業は悪化も一時的な悪天候が主因で変調の懸念は低い
― 業種別では、化学、石油・石炭製品、生産用機械等が改善。旺盛なAI関連需要や原油の供給不足懸念の後退等が背景

― 一方、非製造業は、長雨を受けて小売や対個人サービス、宿泊・飲食サービスが悪化
 2026年度の経常利益計画は、製造業中心に大幅上方修正。9月調査時点としては過去5年間で最高の増益計画
― とりわけ大企業・製造業では、輸出向け売上高の上方修正が顕著。上期の実績が大幅に上振れたことを反映したもの
― 世界的なAI関連需要の好調持続が見込まれるもと、下期も堅調な輸出が続けば、利益計画は一段の上方修正余地

日本：コメ価格下落で企業物価は鈍化。もっとも、先行きは原油高を背景に再加速へ
18
 9月の国内企業物価はコメ価格の下落を主因に、前年比＋6.8％（前月：同＋7.6％）と鈍化

― 一方、3月来の原油高を背景に化学・プラスチック製品は高止まり。また、AI需要等を背景に非鉄金属も高い伸びを維持

く可能性

国内債券・為替：株安を背景に長期金利は低下。ドル円は150円台後半で横ばい圏
 長期金利は、株安を背景に低下。10年金利・30年金利とも、10月上旬から30bps以上の大幅低下
― 米新興クラウドサービスの社債価格急落が株安の契機となり、安全資産とされる日本国債に資金が流入
― 先行き、株式市場の混乱が続けば、短期的には一段の金利低下余地も。もっとも、日銀は四半期に一度のペースで利上
げを続けるとみられ、混乱一巡後の円金利は、再び緩やかな上昇基調に転じる見通し
 ドル円は150円台後半で方向感に欠ける展開。先行きも、150円台を中心とした円安水準が続く見込み
― 原油高は円安要因の一方、株安に伴うリスクオフや仏情勢悪化を受けたユーロ安は円高要因。目先は両者が綱引きへ

内外株式：AIインフラ投資への信用不安の高まりから、AI・半導体関連株を中心に下落
 日米株は一時上昇も、米新興クラウドサービスに対する信用不安などからAI・半導体関連株を中心に下落
― 企業業績への期待から、10月初の日米株は上昇。S&P500は一時最高値を更新。日経平均も一時7万円台を回復
― その後は、米新興クラウドサービスの社債価格の急落を受け、AI・半導体関連株を中心に下落。原油価格の上昇に伴

うインフレ懸念も下押し圧力に。もっとも、S&P500はヘルスケア関連株等への資金シフトが進み、底堅く推移
 AIインフラ投資に対する信用不安は残存。原油価格の上昇も重石となり、日米株は軟調な推移が続くと予想
― 金利が高水準で推移する中、AIインフラの過剰投資懸念が強まり株価を下押しするリスクには留意が必要

如何理解价格数据的回落？，2026 年 8 月
9 日

PPI 环比转负，下半年物价表现或回归基
本面，2026 年7 月9 日

关注 PPI 超预期与CPI 低迷背后的价格传导困局            
      ——2026 年10 月价格数据点评

事件：2026 年10 月全国居民消费价格指数（CPI）同比0.7%（前
值 0.9%），环比 0%（前值 0.2%），1-10 月 CPI 同比 0.9%（前值
0.9%）；核心 CPI 同比 0.8%（前值 1%），环比 0%（前值 0%），1-10
月核心 CPI 同比 1.1%（前值 1.1%）；工业生产者出厂价格指数（PPI）
同比 5.7%（前值 5%），环比 0.7%（前值 1.2%），1-10 月 PPI 同比
2.7%（前值2.3%）。 
食品价格走弱、工业品价格支撑减弱，CPI 环比由正转平,略高于
季节性水平，CPI 同比涨幅边际回落；下半年以来，CPI 累计同比及核
心 CPI 累计同比分别维持在 0.9%和 1.1%，较上半年均回落 0.1 个百分
点，价格修复节奏放缓。10 月 CPI 同比增长 0.7%，较上月回落 0.2 个
百分点，结束连续两个月回升走势，环比 由上月上涨 0.2%转为持平，
强于季节性水平（2023 年-2025 年，10 月CPI 环比均值为-0.1%）。10
月核心 CPI（剔除食品和能源）同比增长 0.8%，较上月回落 0.2 个百
分点，环比 0%，低于季节性水平（2023 年-2025 年，10 月核心 CPI 环
比均值为 0.1%）。1-10 月，CPI 同比及核心 CPI 同比分别为 0.9%和
1.1%，与前三季度持平，较上年底回升，但均较上半年回落 0.1 个百
分点，终端消费需求持续修复但仍偏弱。 
 食品价格 环比由涨转降 ，且低于季节性水平 ，同比降幅 连续两个月
扩大，食品价格偏弱是本月 CPI 的主要拖累。10 月食品价格同比下
降 2.9%，降幅比上月扩大 1.2 个百分点，连续两个月降幅扩大，影
响CPI 同比下降约0.49 个百分，环比由上月上涨0.4%转为下降1.0%，
低于季节性水平（ 2023 年-2025 年，10 月食品价格 环比 均值为-
0.6%），影响 CPI 环比下降约 0.17 个百分点。其中，气候适宜、夏
秋蔬菜供应衔接顺畅 ，蛋类、鲜菜和鲜果价格 环比均由正转负， 10
月环比分别下降 5.2%、5.8%和 0.6%；市场供应充足下，水产品价格
环比降幅扩大、猪肉价格环比由正转负。

2026 年10 月价格数据点评  
关注PPI 超预期与CPI 低迷背后的价格传导困局 
 工业消费品价格支撑 CPI 环比上行但支撑作用有所减弱。10 月工业消费品价格环比上涨 0.3%，
影响CPI 环比上涨约0.10 个百分点（9 月工业消费品影响CPI 环比上涨约0.22 个百分点），
工业消费品对 CPI 的支撑作用减弱。工业消费品中，国内汽油价格上涨 4.2%，影响 CPI 环比
上涨约0.14 个百分点，油价仍是CPI 的重要支撑。受国际金价下行影响，10 月黄金饰品价格
环比下降5.9%，环比连续两个月为负，影响10 月CPI 环比下降约0.03 个百分点,是CPI 环比
修复的重要拖累。 
 假期效应带动下， 服务价格环比降转涨，高于季节性水平，其中宾馆住宿、飞机票等假期相
关消费价格环比均由负转正。10 月，服务价格同比上涨 0.7%，涨幅较上月回落 0.1 个百分点，
或主要受中秋错期影响（今年中秋节在9 月，去年在10 月），服务价格环比由上月下降0.2%
转为上涨0.1%，对CPI 的影响由上月的拖累转为支持，影响CPI 环比上涨约0.04 个百分点，
高于季节性（2023-2025 年 10 月，服务价格环比均值为 0.03%）。具体来看，国庆假期出行
人数增加，宾馆住宿、飞机票和旅行社收费环比均由负转正，10 月份分别上涨 6.5%、3.3%和
2.4%，合计影响CPI 环比上涨约0.04 个百分点。

### Page 3

www.ccxi.com.cn 
2026 年10 月价格数据点评  
关注PPI 超预期与CPI 低迷背后的价格传导困局 
PPI 环比连续三个月正增，10 月 PPI 同比 5.7%、为 2022 年 7 月以来高点。1-10 月 PPI
同比 2.7%，较前值上行 0.4 个百分点，为 2023 年以来的高点。10 月 PPI 同比 5.7%，较上月
上行 0.7 个百分点，PPI 环比 0.7%，连续三个月维持正增。PPI 与 CPI 剪刀差进一步扩大至 5
个百分点，价格传导仍存在阻碍。PPI 分项中，生产资料价格同比与生活资料价格同比延续一
正一负，二者剪刀差边际扩大。其中，生产资料同比7.4%，较前值上行0.9 个百分点，是PPI
同比上行的重要支撑，生活资料同比-0.1%，降幅与上月相同，二者剪刀差较上月扩大 0.9 个
百分点，终端需求偏弱，上下游行业分化加剧。当前 PPI 上涨仍主要集中在资源品行业和部
分高技术行业，下游企业的成本承受能力需重点关注。结合 PPI 与 PPIRM 剪刀差来看，今年
以来，二者剪刀差由平转负，且差值波动扩大，10 月二者剪刀差较前值扩大 0.3 个百分点，
二者分别为 5.7%和 7.9%，PPIRM 上涨的速度显著高于 PPI，企业尤其是中下游企业盈利承压
较大。

### Page 4

3 
www.ccxi.com.cn 
2026 年10 月价格数据点评  
关注PPI 超预期与CPI 低迷背后的价格传导困局 
 生产资料价格环比连续 3 个月维持正增，输入性因素、季节性因素、反内卷深入推进以及人
工智能行业快速发展带动部分相关行业价格上行，是 PPI 环比正增、同比上行的重要支撑。
具体来看，一是 10 月国际油价整体维持高位震荡，中东地缘风险溢价持续支撑油价。受国际
油价波动影响，石油和天然气开采及石油、煤炭及其他燃料加工业价格环比连续 3 个月为正，
10 月分别为 6%和 6.1%，化学原料及化学制品制造业价格环比连续两个月保持正增，10 月为
3.1%，均显著高于全部工业品 PPI 环比增速水平。二是受需求共振与能源冲击影响，煤炭加
工、燃气生产和供应业价格环比均为正，分别为 3.2%和 1.5%，对 PPI 环比改善带来一定支撑。
三是产业治理深入推进， 推动部分行业供求关系持续改善、价格水平持续修复，其中 非金属
矿物制品业（包括水泥、玻璃等）价格环比连续两个月正增，10 月环比上涨 0.8%，汽车制造
业价格环比在连续下降 3 个月后于 10 月份转为持平。四是人工智能持续发展，对 AI 产业链
相关行业价格上行带来助力，其中 电气机械和器材制造业、计算机通信和其他电子设备制造
业价格环比均上涨0.1%，亦对PPI 上行带来支撑。  
 生活资料价格环比由正转负，同比仍为负，终端消费仍偏弱。10 月生活资料价格同比降幅与
9 月相同，均为-0.1%，环比由正转负，显示当前生活资料价格仍偏弱。从分项来看，衣着类、
一般日用品类价格环比均由平转负，食品类价格环比由正转平，耐用消费品类价格环比由正
转负。结合 CPI 分项中耐用消费品价格来看，以家电为主的家用器具价格环比连续 3 个月为
负且降幅逐月扩大，显示以旧换新政策对耐用消费品价格支撑作用持续减弱。 
图 6: 10 月 PPI 同比上行 图 7： PPI 翘尾因素

### Page 5

4 
www.ccxi.com.cn 
2026 年10 月价格数据点评  
关注PPI 超预期与CPI 低迷背后的价格传导困局

整体来看， 10 月物价走势延续分化， 受输入性和季节性因素带动主要能源价格上涨等
多因素影响，PPI 同比涨幅创2022 年下半年以来新高，显著高于此前市场预期的5.3%左右，
其中石油和天然气开采业、煤炭开采和洗选业、精炼石油产品制造、有机化学原料制造、煤
炭加工、燃气生产和供应业等能源相关 6 个行业合计影响 PPI 环比上涨约 0.61 个百分点，
超过总涨幅的八成。但与此同时，CPI 同比涨幅回落、生活资料 PPI 同比和环比均为负，需
求偏弱下，上下游价格分化仍在加剧，价格传导困局仍存。后续来看，输入性因素仍是短期
影响物价走势的关键变量之一，11 月初，美国中期选举落定，民主党拿下参众两院，同时伊
朗释放和谈信号，地缘担忧有所回落，国际油价回落，截至11 月6 日，WTI 原油期货结算价
已有 10 月底的 104 美元/桶回落至 90.8 美元/桶，但仍位于较高水平。同时迎峰度冬带来煤、
气阶段性需求增加，PPI 同比短期维持较高水平仍有支撑。不过若后续国际能源价格涨幅延
续回落，叠加考虑去年四季度基数波动走高，PPI 上行斜率或边际放缓。CPI 方面，蔬菜随
降温进入季节性涨价窗口，但生猪供给仍偏充裕，猪价缺乏持续大涨基础，食品价格短期仍
偏弱。此外，长假期结束，服务价格短期面临回落压力，对 CPI 的支撑或减弱。整体来看，
短期 CPI 大概率维持低位温和运行，实现年初2%的目标存在较大压力。

2026年 10月 30日 全7頁

Indicators Update 
2026年 9月鉱工業生産 
自動車工業が挽回生産で持ち直し、生産全体は3カ月ぶりに上昇

経済調査部 エコノミスト ビリング 安奈

[要約] 
◼ 2026年 9月の生産指数は前月比＋2.2％と、コンセンサス（同＋1.2％、Bloomberg調
査）を上回り、3 カ月ぶりに上昇した。自動車工業は挽回生産で持ち直したほか、AI
関連需要で生産用機械工業なども増産し、生産全体を押し上げた。経済産業省は基調
判断を「一進一退」に据え置いた。 
◼ 先行きの生産指数は緩やかに上昇する見込み。AI関連需要などが引き続き国内生産を
押し上げよう。ただし、金利高による海外需要の下振れなどが生産を下押しするリス
クには注意が必要だ。 
◼ 2026年 11月 9日に公表予定の 9月分の景気動向指数は、先行 CIが前月差▲0.9ptの
117.1、一致CIが同＋0.7ptの 119.5と予想する。この予測値に基づくと、9月の基調
判断は機械的に「改善」に据え置かれる。

### Page 2

2 / 7

【生産】自動車工業は挽回生産で持ち直し、AI関連需要で生産用機械工業も増産 
2026年 9月の生産指数は前月比＋2.2％と、コンセンサス（同＋1.2％、Bloomberg調査）を
上回り、3カ月ぶりに上昇した。経済産業省は基調判断を「一進一退」に据え置いた。 
生産指数を業種別に見ると、15業種中 8業種が前月から上昇した。自動車工業（前月比＋
5.5％）は 2カ月ぶりに上昇した。品目別では、普通乗用車（同＋3.3％）や駆動伝導・操縦装
置部品（同＋7.0％）などが増産となった。前月は熊本地震や台風による工場稼働停止や部品
供給の停滞を受けて生産が落ち込んだが、9 月は生産体制がおおむね正常化したことで挽回生
産が進み、自動車工業の生産は持ち直した。また、生産用機械工業（同＋3.4％）は4カ月連続
で上昇した。品目別では、AI関連製品である半導体製造装置（同＋13.8％）のほか、化学機械
（同＋34.9％）などが増産となった。経済産業省「鉱工業出荷内訳表」によると、生産用機械
工業は国内向け（同＋7.8％）が好調だった。汎用・業務用機械工業（同＋4.8％）では、コン
ベヤ（同＋142.0％）や運搬用クレーン（同＋35.5％）が増産となり、2カ月ぶりの上昇となっ
た。一方、鉄鋼・非鉄金属工業（前月比▲1.6％）や窯業・土石製品工業（同▲1.9％）など 7
業種は前月から低下した。鉄鋼・非鉄金属工業では亜鉛めっき鋼板（同▲10.0％）などが、窯
業・土石製品工業ではガラス繊維（短繊維）（同▲6.3％）などが減産となった。 
財別では、生産財（前月比＋1.0％）、耐久消費財（同＋5.3％）、非耐久消費財（同＋0.9％）、
建設財（同＋2.5％）、資本財（除．輸送機械） （同＋1.0％）のいずれも増産となった。設備投
資の関連指標である資本財（除．輸送機械）はAI需要を背景に大幅な増産が見込まれる。

【出荷・在庫】出荷指数は自動車工業などを中心に2カ月ぶりに上昇 
2026年 9月の出荷指数は前月比＋0.6％と 2カ月ぶりに上昇した。業種別では、自動車工業
（同＋2.3％）や汎用・業務用機械工業（同＋5.5％）など 15業種中 8業種が上昇した。財別に
見ると、非耐久消費財と資本財（除．輸送機械）、建設財は低下した一方、耐久消費財と生産
財は上昇した。在庫指数は同＋2.5％、在庫率指数は同＋2.0％だった。

【先行き】生産指数は緩やかに上昇する見込み 
先行きの生産指数は、緩やかな上昇基調を辿るとみている。AI関連需要などが引き続き国内
生産の押し上げ要因となる。ただし、世界的な金利高、特に米国の金利高による海外需要の下
振れなどが生産を下押しするリスクには注意が必要だ。 
AI関連財の拡大は 2024年から日本国内の生産を押し上げてきており、今後も生産の押し上
げ要因になると見込まれる1。26年 8月の世界半導体販売額（3カ月平均）を見ると、前年比＋
144.3％と強い伸びが続いている。米国の販売額（同＋180.9％）がけん引する中、日本（同＋
62.3％）2も力強さを示している。また、日本半導体製造装置協会によると、26年9月の半導体
製造装置販売高（3カ月平均）は同＋48.2％と高い伸びとなった3。AI関連を中心とする旺盛な
半導体需要が反映されているとみられる。ただし、米国の金利高でAI関連需要が下振れする可
能性には注意が必要だ。高金利下で米企業の資金調達負担が増え、多額の資金を必要とする AI
関連投資が鈍化すれば4、日本の生産も輸出減を通して下押しされるリスクがある。 
なお、中東情勢による悪影響は引き続き注視する必要がある。米国の中間選挙後に対イラン
攻撃が再開される可能性も報じられており5、原油供給をめぐる不安は引き続き残る。一方、今
年 2月末に始まった中東危機を受け、ホルムズ海峡の輸送停滞への対応として政府が国家備蓄
原油を放出して以降、初めて買い戻しが実施された6。代替調達が進み、国家備蓄原油の取り崩
しが一服し、備蓄の補充が始まったことは好材料だ。ただし、情勢が再び悪化すれば、原材料
不足を通じて生産が下押しされる可能性がある。 
製造工業生産予測調査では、10月の生産指数は前月比＋5.1％と見込まれる。業種別では 11
業種中 9業種が上昇する見通しだ。電子部品・デバイス工業（同＋16.6％）で集積回路などの
増産が計画されているほか、生産用機械工業（同＋15.5％）では半導体・フラットパネルディ
スプレイ製造装置など、AI関連製品の増産が全体を押し上げる見通しだ。生産指数全体の計画
のバイアスを補正した試算値（最頻値）7で見ると、10月は同＋3.9％と上昇が見込まれる。 
11月の生産指数は前月比＋0.8％と見込まれている。業種別では 11業種中 7業種が上昇する
見通しだ。輸送機械工業（同＋4.3％）では乗用車など、汎用・業務用機械工業（同＋4.8％）
では計測・分析機器などの増産が見込まれている。

1 詳細は、神田慶司・秋元虹輝・菊池慈陽・横田凱「日本経済見通し：2026年6月」（大和総研レポート、
2026年6月23日）を参照。 
2 World Semiconductor Trade Statistics “Historical Billings Report”（最終閲覧日：2026年10月 30
日） 
3 日本半導体製造装置協会「2026年9月度販売高（SEAJ速報値）日本製半導体製造装置（3ヶ月平均）」
（2026年10月 23日） 
4 詳細は、矢作大祐・藤原翼「米国経済見通し CoreWeave問題をどうみるか」（大和総研レポート、2026年
10月22日）を参照。 
5 「トランプ政権が湾岸諸国に対イラン攻撃への準備を要請 米中間選挙後に最終判断か」（産経新聞、2026
年 10月 29日）

28日）

【26年9月景気動向指数】先行CIは低下、一致CIは上昇を見込む 
鉱工業指数の結果を受けて、2026年 11月 9日に公表予定の 9月分の景気動向指数は、先行
CIが前月差▲0.9ptの 117.1、一致 CIが同＋0.7ptの 119.5と予想する（図表 4） 。先行CIで
は構成指標のうち、鉱工業用生産財在庫率指数（逆サイクル）や最終需要財在庫率指数（逆サ
イクル）、東証株価指数などが前月から悪化した。一致 CIでは構成指標のうち、生産指数（鉱
工業）や鉱工業用生産財出荷指数、有効求人倍率（除学卒）などが改善した。この予測値に基
づくと、2026年9月の基調判断は機械的に「改善」に据え置かれる。

## Document 153: 261103_daiwa_Realtek

Source PDF: all report pdf/261103_daiwa_Realtek.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-03
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: Following our results memo, we fine-tune our forecasts for 
Realtek. We believe its near-term demand-rebalancing hiccups have come 
to an end, thanks to its pricing power and its ability to pass on supply-chain 
cost inflation. Given our interpretation of management’s tone that 4Q26 is 
likely to be sequentially flat, we expect Realtek’s business to return to its 
seasonal track from 1Q27. We expect it to continue capitalising on its 
structure growth driver of multi-year/multi-G (gigabits per second) 
connectivity bandwidth upgrade cycle, with any AI penetration adding to 
upside. We reaffirm Outperform (2) call with a higher 12M TP of TWD798.

What’s the impact: Forecast revisions. We fine-tune our 2026-28E EPS 
given that fundamentals are broadly intact heading into 2027. Recall 
management’s commentary at its results call (29 October) of expecting 
4Q26 to be a “prudent and steady” quarter. We interpret this as likely a 
sequentially flat revenue, counter-seasonal after its 3Q26 miss on deeper 
demand rebalancing. Without quantifying its 2027 business outlook, 
management expects the year to be one of steady growth. We forecast 
Realtek’s 2027 revenue to grow by 18% YoY, assuming its business returns 
to seasonal track after 2 years of demand rebalancing hiccups caused by 
customers’ unusual restocking and pre-stocking activities.

Back on track after recent hiccups. After 2 years of anti-seasonal 
performance on customer pre-stocking amid uncertainty over Trump’s 
reciprocal tariffs, followed by component price hikes and supply-chain 
uncertainty related to AI cannibalisation, we expect Realtek’s business to 
return to its seasonal track from 1Q27. We believe it should continue to 
capitalise on its secular driver of multi-year/multi-G bandwidth upgrade 
cycle from both wireline and wireless connectivity, including 2.5G/5G/10G 
Ethernet, 10GPON, managed switches and WiFi 7/8. Its penetration into AI, 
from cloud AI companion chips to edge AI to physical AI, should add to 
growth upside, although any meaningful contribution may take time, 
evidenced by its new product offering/development such as SSD, Ethernet 
hub, optical cable, i3C security and SerDes controllers, to name a few. 
According to management, AI will contribute a high single-digit % revenue 
this year, ramping to over 30% contribution by 2030.

What we recommend: Following forecast revisions, we raise our 12M TP 
for Realtek to TWD798 (previous: TWD750), on the same 4-quarter forward 
PER target of 20x. We reaffirm our Outperform (2) rating with key downside 
risk of any component price hikes leading to worse-than-expected demand.

How we differ: Our 2026-28E EPS are broadly in line with consensus, 
ranging from -3% to +2%.

3 November 2026

Back on track after rebalancing

➢ Fine-tuning our forecasts on demand rebalancing after pre-stocking 
➢ Likely back on seasonal track after this year’s hiccups 
➢ Reaffirming Outperform (2) call with a higher 12-month TP of TWD798

Company profile 
Realtek focuses on research, design, development and distribution of integrated circuits (ICs) for 
applications across networking & communication (NC), computer & peripheral (CP) and multimedia & 
consumer (MM). The majority of its revenue comes from NC products, including WiFi, Bluetooth, 
Ethernet and switch controllers. It also provides diversified CP products such as audio codec, IP cam, 
and solid-state drive (SSD) controllers as well as MM products like TV SoC and monitor controllers.

### Page 5

5

Realtek Semiconductor (2379 TT): 3 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 29 Jan 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
Realtek’s Board of Directors consists of nine members whose expertise spans industry,
academia, finance, accounting, and management, providing the company with a broad and
diverse range of professional perspectives. Three of these directors are independent,
representing 33 percent of the board and exceeding the regulatory minimum requirement of 20
percent for independent directors. This higher proportion of independent directors enhances
oversight, helps safeguard shareholder interests, and supports more objective decision‑making.
However, an important governance risk remains in the area of board diversity. The board
currently includes only one female director out of nine members, which represents 11 percent of
the total. This limited female representation may constrain the breadth of viewpoints and
experiences reflected in board discussions and could be perceived as misaligned with evolving
stakeholder expectations and best practices on gender diversity in corporate governance.
Capital management 1
In the past 8 years, Realtek's dividend payout ratios were above 80%. As a fabless company, it
does not need high capital expenditure for manufacturing sites. Realtek's business has shown
stable earnings growth over its 30-year history. Thus, we see its dividend policy with a high
payout ratio as appropriate.
Related party &
transaction 2
Realtek’s related-party transactions are primarily with G.M.I. Technology Inc. and Greatek
Electronics Inc. Realtek pays certain processing costs to related parties. These payments are
made under standard commercial terms and conditions. Overall, we do not observe any
significant impact on Realtek’s business arising from these related-party transactions.
S Supply chain management 2
Realtek has formulated a set of supplier management regulations in accordance with Realtek's
Supplier Sustainability Management Policy. It requires suppliers to obtain ISO9001 quality
management system certification to ensure that all of its suppliers possess a high level of
environment awareness.
E Water & wastewater management 1
As a fabless company without manufacturing plants, Realtek does not produce much wastewater
compared to manufacturing companies, and thus has a relatively limited impact on the
environment. Realtek has implemented a water conservation plan to increase water efficiency,
such as the reuse of rainwater, re-circulating water in its cooling tower and installing water-
saving devices. Its water saving rate (=reduced water consumption/total water withdrawn)
increased gradually.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
1
The company reports to the Executive Yuan's Environmental Protection Administration waste
and reporting management system every month on the quantity of waste and its method of
disposal. In 2024, it produced 26.10 tonnes of industrial waste, maintaining over a 90% recycling
rate for hazardous industrial waste.
E GHG emissions 1
Realtek has independently conducted GHG emission inventory and management at its Hsinchu
Headquarters since 2019. In 2022, Realtek implemented a comprehensive ISO 14064-1:2018
GHG inventory and passed third-party verification. As of 2024, the company has completed
GHG emission inventory and third-party verification for all sites within the Realtek Group.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

Greater China Technology Hardware

Industry View In-Line
Results:
• AT&S (ATSV.VI, Not Covered) reported C3Q revenue of €682mn (+24% q/q):
Electronics Solutions (+26% q/q) and Microelectronics (ABF; +23% q/q).
• C3Q/F2Q Microelectronics (ABF) EBIT margin increased another 580bp q/q,
to 30.5%, which management attributed to higher volumes, better product

mix and an improved pricing environment.
Guidance:
• Revenue: +45-55% y/y, unchanged.
• EBITDA margin: +32-37%, unchanged.
• AT&S expects its ongoing capacity expansion for the Microelectronics
business to have a positive impact on revenue and earnings already in the
current fiscal year.
Capacity expansion:
• In June (link), AT&S announced the expansion of its production site in Kulim,
based on agreements with its customer AMD (covered by Joseph Moore) and

another leading technology company.
• AT&S reaffirmed that the planned investments of €1.5–2.0bn, supported by
long-term customer commitments, will provide additional capacity to meet
growing demand for advanced IC substrates.
• In September (link), AT&S announced an expanded collaboration with
Marvell (covered by Joseph Moore) to further increase advanced IC substrate
capacity for next-generation AI and cloud infrastructure as well. Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
Microelectronics EBIT margin up another 580bp q/q, to 30.5%, partly from
pricing; positive read for ABF peers Unimicron and NYPCB.
Customers'  commitments underpin €1.5-2.0bn Kulim expansion. Signals tight
supply, multi-year visibility.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Unimicron (3037.TW)
Base case, residual income (RI) valuation model, which we think derives the most accurate
value of the firm given that it takes into account cost of equity. We use a cost of equity of 9.2%
[risk-free rate of 1% (10-year Taiwan government note yield), equity risk premium of 8.7%, and
a beta of 1.0], a medium-term growth rate of 14%, and a terminal growth rate of 3%.

Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected ABF substrate demand from PC and server customers
n Capex cuts; halt to its capacity expansion plan
n Continued yield issues of alternative technology that doesn't require substrate (e.g.,
CoWoP)
Risks to Downside
n Sudden demand shortfall
n Technological change that would not require ABF substrates
n Intensifying competition
n Yield issues or production hiccups when ramping new capacity

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

## Document 210: 261109_daiwa_ Taiwan Consumer Discretionary Global PMX

Source PDF: all report pdf/261109_daiwa_ Taiwan Consumer Discretionary Global PMX.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-09
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Consumer Discretionary

What’s new: We attended PMX’s investor conference after the company 
released its 3Q26 results on 9 November 2026. We reaffirm our Buy (1) call 
as we see better-than-expected demand for its semiconductor, AI server 
and medical segments.

What’s the impact: 3Q26 results missed mainly on FX loss. PMX’s 
3Q26 revenue of TWD2,417m (+5.9% QoQ, +24.6% YoY) beat our 
estimate by 8.5% and was in line with the Bloomberg consensus. Its gross 
margin came in at 23.9% vs. 24.6% in 2Q26 and 21.7% in 3Q25, missing 
our estimate (24.2%) and the consensus (25.1%), mainly due to some one-
off costs ramping up at the new Vietnam plant. With higher-than-expected 
opex, operating profit of TWD340m (+10.0% QoQ, +54.4% YoY) missed 
our and the consensus estimates by 1.3% and 6.0%, respectively. With an 
FX loss of TWD23m, its net profit of TWD271m (+13.3% QoQ, +0.1% YoY) 
missed our and consensus estimates by 11.1% and 10.2%, respectively. 
EPS was TWD2.36 in 3Q26. PMX’s 3Q26 revenue breakdown is as 
follows: auto sales at 49% (+13% YoY), semiconductor, AI server & other 
sales at 20% (+66% YoY), storage device sales at 13% (+25% YoY), 
medical sales at 10% (+13% YoY), and sports sales at 8% (+42% YoY). 
PMX also posted October 2026 monthly sales of TWD816m (+3.3% MoM 
and +33.8% YoY; +22.0% YoY for 10M26), which accounted for 35.6% and 
31.3%, respectively, of our previous and the consensus forecasts of 4Q26 
revenue.

Segment transformation continues with upbeat guidance for 4Q26 
and 2027. For 2026, PMX maintained its overall 20-25% YoY revenue 
growth guidance with semiconductor, AI server & others segment growing 
by at least 100% YoY. For 2027, management expects revenue to grow by 
20%+ YoY with the semiconductor, AI server & others segment to grow by 
100%+ YoY; medical, by 50%+ YoY; storage device, by 10% YoY; auto, flat; 
and sports to decline by 5%. We revised down our 2026-28E EPS by 0.3-
8.5% after factoring in its 3Q26 results.

What we recommend: We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating with an unchanged 
12M TP of TWD205, now based on a target PER of 17x (from 18x; above 
the 15x average of its past-3-year range of 11-25x) on our 1-year forward 
EPS forecast. We lower the PER target slightly on its decelerating earnings 
momentum. Key downside risks: lower than-expected global auto, medical, 
and AI server component demand; worse-than-expected gross margin.

How we differ: Our 2026-27E EPS are 18-22% below the Bloomberg 
consensus, likely as we are more conservative about PMX’s revenue and 
operating margins.

9 November 2026

3Q26 results review: weaker-than-expected margins

➢ 3Q26 net profit missed our and consensus estimates by 10-11% 
➢ Semiconductor, AI server and Others segments to support growth  
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) call with unchanged 12M TP of TWD205

Company profile 
Founded in 1987, Global PMX provides mechanical parts for automotive, medical equipment, and 
storage device components. Its major automotive clients include Continental, Bosch, BorgWarner, ZF, 
Hilite and Tesla. Other major clients are Seagate, Medtronic, and Zimmer.

### Page 5

5

Global PMX (4551 TT): 9 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 8 May 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
The CEO and Chairperson of PMX are not the same individual. The board includes 3
independent directors, including 1 female independent director. The Chairman has held this
position at PMX for more than 20 years, indicating the company's strong management capability
and leadership, in our view.
Capital management 1
PMX's payout ratio was 80-90% before 2018, but dropped to 29-69% in 2019-25, which we
attribute to its acquisitions over the past few of years (eg, merger with Sixxon in 2020). We view
these acquisitions as reasonable capital management as PMX’s product line expansion has led
to lower risk exposure due to diversification, helping PMX's 2020-24 revenue to stay relatively
intact.
Related party &
transaction 2 No major revenue from related-party transactions in 2020-25, and we observed no severe issues.
S Product quality & safety 2
PMX holds numerous international product quality certifications, including ISO9002, QS9000 and
ISO/TS16949, which help to regulate the production phases, from development to quality control
and sales. Besides, products made by PMX are 100% compliant with laws/regulations and
market expectations on health and safety, so we believe that product quality and safety concerns
are not a high risk for PMX.
S Materials sourcing & efficiency 2 Global PMX's supply conditions of raw materials (mainly copper and steel) are stable, and its suppliers are required to sign the company’s Supplier Commitment Statement. Therefore, we believe materials sourcing and efficiency will be under control going forward.
S Product design & lifecycle management 2 PMX has adopted strategies that avoid price competition by targeting production of higher value- added products (including auto, medical, semiconductor, and green energy products). Thus, we believe that PMX is continually improving its product lifecycle management.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

## Document 231: 5 risks for the global economy in 2027

Source PDF: all report pdf/5 risks for the global economy in 2027.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-10
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From AI bubbles to geopolitical breakthroughs, these are the
ﬁve ways our outlook could go wrong – or right

Carsten Brzeski and James Smith
US stocks plunged on 5 November 2026 as AI and chip stocks sold oﬀ, pushing
the Nasdaq into correction territory
A severe correction in US stocks
triggered by an AI meltdown
The US tech correction extends into a full-blown, deep bear market.
Why? Demand for Artiﬁcial Intelligence disappoints, perhaps
because of weak enterprise adoption. Cheaper models break
through at a time when corporates grow wary of pricey tokens.
Overcapacity builds and higher ﬁnancing costs bite across the
industry, triggering credit events outside the major hyperscalers.
US activity takes a big hit; around a third of growth has come from
AI so far in 2026. As a rule of thumb, a further 30% equity
drawdown would wipe out $24.5tr of household wealth. This is
concentrated among high-income consumers who are less likely to
cut back on spending. Consumers barely ﬂinched when the dotcom

### Page 2

bubble popped, but this time those high earners are the only ones
spending.
Investment would bear the bigger brunt. Investment in data centres
and infrastructure is an obvious risk, but tighter ﬁnancing
conditions – via wider credit spreads – would hit businesses well
beyond AI. Non-tech investment took a bigger hit post-dotcom
than tech itself.
The good news is this is not 2008; household and business balance
sheets are much healthier. The Fed would also likely deliver sharp

rate cuts. Chair Warsh is not convinced that policy is currently
restrictive, no doubt because equity markets are supported. But if
that changes, a key pillar of the Fed’s tightening cycle falls away.
Impact: Potential US recession, while Europe is less aﬀected.
Curtailed Fed hiking cycle and faster rate cuts.
Second-round inﬂation effects emerge at
scale
One of our main calls across Europe is that second-round eﬀects
from the energy shock remain contained, allowing major central

banks to cut rates toward the end of 2027. But this could be wrong
for four reasons.
First, the lack of second-round eﬀects so far simply reﬂects longer
lag times. Businesses have built more resilience to shocks since
2022, via hedging (think European airlines) and supply chains. But
as the crisis lasts longer, this resilience is increasingly challenged.
The recent spike in natural gas prices also means this is no longer
solely an oil crisis. That could see a dramatic turnaround in food

inﬂation through the winter. The big test could be price resets in the
new year across the services sector.
Second, the jobs market starts to tighten. Vacancies increase at a
time when labour supply is constrained by low migration in many
countries. Workers gain the upper hand in wage bargaining
processes and beneﬁt from bigger pay settlements and/or one-oﬀ
bonuses. That ampliﬁes the impact on services inﬂation.

### Page 3

Third, energy prices themselves spike higher. Prolonged escalation
in the Middle East could take oil to $130/bbl or above this winter.
Central banks have explicitly argued that energy prices and the
probability of second-round eﬀects are positively correlated.
Finally, governments start to bear the cost of the energy shock on
their balance sheets, boosting support to households and
businesses, despite rising borrowing costs. That would ease the hit

to growth. And though it could temper the rise in headline inﬂation
if support directly lowers energy costs, it would add to inﬂationary
pressures over the medium term.
Impact: Core inﬂation remains well above target in 2027. More
signiﬁcant rate hikes, proving market expectations right. Rate cuts
delayed.
Weather and critical infrastructure
failures shock the global economy
This decade has proved that supply shocks are becoming more
common, supporting our long-held view that inﬂation cycles are

getting shorter. There are two obvious shocks that could strike in
2027.
First, weather. El Niño, if particularly severe, could dramatically
increase agricultural prices (though our research suggests this isn’t
inevitable). Low water levels in the Panama Canal could also pose a
wildcard for shipping and supply chains. Overlaid on the existing
energy shock, headline inﬂation could remain at 4%, especially in
Europe, well into 2028.
Second, a major critical infrastructure failure. A cyberattack or

cascading technical failure disables electricity, payments,
communications or cloud services across multiple European
economies for several days. The economic damage is likely to rise
non-linearly with the duration, frequency and geographical reach of
an outage. The 18-hour power outage across much of Spain in
2025 had little discernible impact on GDP. But repeated, high-
impact and unpredictable outages would hit conﬁdence, and also

require ﬁrms to divert signiﬁcant sums to building resilience, via
more complex supply chains, better systems and hedging.
Impact: Lower growth and potentially higher inﬂation, especially if
there is a commodity price aspect to these shocks. Central banks
are more likely to lean hawkish.
Geopolitical de-escalation delivers an
energy peace dividend
With the Republicans in a weakened state post the midterms and
President Trump increasingly focused on his legacy, a US-driven

settlement ends the Ukraine war earlier than expected and
facilitates a broader accommodation with Russia. Washington
pressures Kyiv to accept territorial compromises in return for
security guarantees, while oﬀering Moscow phased sanctions relief
and renewed economic co-operation in exchange for a durable
ceaseﬁre.
Improved US-Russia relations, in turn, create scope for faster, wider
Middle East diplomacy, including Russian support for a negotiated

settlement with Iran and tighter nuclear constraints. Faster-than-
expected de-escalation across both theatres reduces immediate
European security risks and disruption to energy and shipping,
while supporting conﬁdence, investment and Ukraine’s
reconstruction.
Impact: A signiﬁcant and durable fall in oil (and crucially, diesel)
and European natural gas prices, via a lower risk premium and later
improved ﬂows. Inﬂation stays high in the ﬁrst quarter, given the

pressure already in the pipeline, but falls below 2% by summer in
the US and Europe. Central bank rate cuts are accelerated.
The bond market rout gets even worse
Our Rates Strategy team argues that US 10-year yields could hit
6% if US growth accelerates to 3-4%, the ﬁscal deﬁcit gets even
worse, or inﬂation fails to come down.
Which of those factors dominate clearly matters; a positive
demand shock clearly has very diﬀerent ramiﬁcations from the

Regardless, even higher yields – particularly in a volatile bond

French spreads blow out beyond 200bp and that seeps into other

The ECB faces a dilemma, but signs of more widespread contagion

sounding (or acting) more dovish, inﬂation permitting, or with an

however.

rooted in higher inﬂation.

## Document 244: 8028昇陽半 國票

Source PDF: all report pdf/8028昇陽半 國票.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-04
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

請參閱末頁之聲明

2026.11.04 
徐凰原 C00259@ibfs.com.tw 
8028.TT 昇陽半導體 
 2027 年毛利率大幅上修

[公司拜訪]   
   公司簡介 
昇陽半導體(PSI)成立於 1997 年，以可重複使用的晶圓材料-再生晶圓起家，提供晶圓
加工及晶圓薄化兩大事業項目。以技術創新與品質優先的敬業精神，拓展全新的測試
晶圓並開發寬能隙半導體的薄化技術，使客戶享有更具競爭力的全方位服務  
投資評等/目標價 重點摘要 
買進 284 
【 前 次 投 資 建 議 】買進，305 
【大盤指數/股價】43308 / 214

➢ 3Q26 毛利率符合預期 
2Q26 營收 14.27 億元(QoQ+9.5%)伴隨著產能開出營收/出貨量都創下
歷史新高，受惠於規模經濟放大影響，2Q26 毛利率 37.89%較前季增加
0.69pp，EPS 1.71 元約略符合預期，3Q26 營收 15.32 億元(QoQ＋
7.36%) ，毛利率 37.2% ，稅後獲利 3.37 億元， QoQ+9.15% ，
YoY+67.01%，EPS 1.84 元。 
➢ 4Q26 營運開始加溫，2026 年財報仍可達標 
以目前市況來看，再生晶圓產能利用率仍滿載，昇陽半 導體為少數近年
來積極配合台積電 (2330)擴產的廠商將直接受惠，但受到機台交期影
響，昇陽半導體 2026 年底產能下修到 110 萬片/月，主要產能開出時間
點在 Q4，昇陽半導體也藉由流程優化提升產出，國票預估 4Q26 營收
為 16.40 億元(QoQ＋7.05%)，毛利率 37.7%，稅後獲利 3.74 億元，
QoQ+11.24%，YoY+66.66%，EPS 2.04 元，由於目前匯率優於預期，
昇陽半導體 2026 年毛利率仍可達到 37.59%的優異水準 (薄化優於預
期)，EPS 僅略微下修到 7.20 元(原估 7.30 元)。 
➢ 被訂單追得跑，中港二廠產能將提前開出 
再生晶圓目前利用率呈現滿載主要受惠於 1)先進製程對再生晶圓不管是
質＆量都增加，HBM 相關 Carrier wafer 需求也在增加，2)台積電未來
月需求大於 200 萬片，且降低自身 in-house 比重。3)台積電海外大幅
擴廠，海外廠升級也帶動再生晶圓較先前進一步上修，昇陽半 導體中港
二廠(預估月產能>60 萬片)將提前於 3Q27 開出，預估中港廠最終產能
可達 120 萬片以上 。 國票預估2026 年/2027 年 EPS 分別為 7.20 元/10.15
元的優異水準，分別年增 64.76% / 40.97%。 
➢ 維持昇陽半導體買進評等，目標價 284 元 
伴隨著台積電大幅海外擴廠以及 2nm 擴充進度 ， 昇陽半導體市佔率將由
2024 年的 38%提升到 2028 年的 45%， 毛利率也伴隨著自動化/規模經
濟而大幅改善 ， 我們認為昇陽半導體為少數受惠於 2nm 製程的個股 ， 投
資價值浮現 ， 近5 年 PE 區間約為 21X~51X(晶圓薄化轉虧為盈 ， 且未來
毛利率將改善)，目前 2026 年 PE 約 26.3X，投資評等維持買進評等，
目標價 284 元(2027F PE 28X)。

3Q26 毛利率符合預期 
2Q26 營收 14.27 億元(QoQ+9.5%)伴隨著產能開出營收/出貨量都創下歷史新高，受
惠於規模經濟放大影響，2Q26 毛利率 37.89%較前季增加 0.69pp，EPS 1.71 元約略
符合預期，3Q26 營收 15.32 億元(QoQ＋7.36%)，毛利率 37.2%，稅後獲利 3.37 億
元，QoQ+9.15%，YoY+67.01%，EPS 1.84 元。 
4Q26 營運開始加溫，2026 年財報仍可達標 
以目前市況來看，再生晶圓產能利用率仍滿載，昇陽半 導體為少數近年來積極配合台
積電擴產的廠商將直接受惠，但受到機台交期影響，昇陽半 導體 2026 年底產能下修
到 110 萬片/月，主要產能開出時間點在 Q4，昇陽半導體也藉由流程優化提升產出，
國票預估 4Q26 營收為 16.40 億元(QoQ＋7.05%)，毛利率 37.7%，稅後獲利 3.74 億
元，QoQ+11.24%，YoY+66.66%，EPS 2.04 元，由於目前匯率優於預期，昇陽半導
體 2026 年毛利率仍可達到 37.59%的優異水準(薄化優於預期)，EPS 僅略微下修到 7.20
元(原估 7.30 元)。 
被訂單追得跑，中港二廠產能將提前開出 
再生晶圓目前利用率呈現滿載主要受惠於 1)先進製程對再生晶圓不管是質＆量都增
加，HBM 相關 Carrier wafer 需求也在增加，2)台積電未來月需求大於 200 萬片，且
降低自身 in-house 比重。3)台積電海外大幅擴廠，海外廠升級也帶動再生晶圓較先前
進一步上修，昇陽半導體中港二廠(預估月產能>60 萬片)將提前於 3Q27 開出，預估
中港廠最終產能可達 120 萬片以上。相關同業方面：RS 計畫在 2026 年投資 28 億日
圓、將台灣 12 吋再生晶圓月產能提高至 33 萬片；2027 年投資 124 億日圓，將在台
灣打造第 2 工廠 ， 目標將台灣12 吋再生晶圓月產能提高至 42 萬片(第 1 工廠 37 萬片 、
第 2 工廠 5 萬片)；2028 年將投資 95 億日圓、 將台灣月產能進一步擴增至57 萬片(第
1 工廠 37 萬片、第 2 工廠 20 萬片，RS 計畫在 2026 年~2028 年期間合計投資超過
173 億日圓(分別為 119 億日圓、54 億日圓、未定)擴充日本 12 吋再生晶圓產能，目
標在 2026 年將日本 12 吋再生晶圓月產能提高至 38 萬片(2025 年為 34 萬片)、2027
年提高至 47 萬片，2028 年進一步提高至 52 萬片、在中國的部分，RS 計畫在 2028
年投資 100 億日圓、將中國 12 吋再生晶圓月產能提高至 10 萬片(目前為 5 萬片)。以
目前的投資計畫來看，屆時總產能為 119 萬片，中砂目前月產能為 40 萬片，不過在
記憶體相關市佔率優於昇陽半導體，國票預估 2026 年/2027 年 EPS 分別為 7.20 元
/10.15 元的優異水準，分別年增 64.76% /40.97%。 
預晶圓薄化有利於產品組合 
DrMOS 主要用在 CPU/GPU/ASIC 的 VRM 多相供電。AI 加速器功耗從傳統伺服器等
級一路往 700W、1,000W 以上走，電流越來越大，在用量翻倍方面，傳統通用伺服器
僅需 16 至 32 顆 DrMOS 供電給主晶片與記憶體 ， 而主流8-GPU 架構的 AI 伺服器(如
HGX H100、B200)因單晶片功耗動輒 700W 至 1,000W，供電相數大幅增加，單台用
量一舉攀升至 320 至 400 顆以上 ， 成長逾10 倍 ； 若進入整機櫃架構(如 GB200/GB300 
NVL72) ，整櫃 DrMOS 需求量 更高達 數千顆 。同 時，市 場規格 主流正 從過 往的  
50A–90A 升級至 100A–130A+的高階 SPS，帶動產品單價(ASP)與市場產值雙雙飛
升，目前昇陽半導體的薄化事業部受惠於 DrMOS 的需求增溫，產能利用率大幅度提
升，昇陽半導體當前晶圓薄化月產能約 4 萬片。

## Document 267: [005380] 현대차(005380) 4분기 반등 기대현대차(005380) 4분기 반등 기대4분기 반등 기대 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005380] 현대차(005380) 4분기 반등 기대현대차(005380) 4분기 반등 기대4분기 반등 기대 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

현대차 (005380)

EARNINGS PREVIEW Company Analysis | 자동차/로봇 | 2026.10.16  
4분기 반등 기대

3Q26 Preview 
현대차는 3Q26E 매출액 42.4조원(-9.4% YoY, -13.9% QoQ), 영업이익 2.5조원(-
2.8% YoY, -13.5% QoQ, OPM 5.8%)을 기록, 컨센서스를 하회할 전망이다. 각 부문
별 영업이익은 자동차 1.7조원(-2.0% YoY, -14.9% QoQ, OPM 4.8%), 금융 5,501억
원(-4.5% YoY, -27.5% QoQ, OPM 6.9%)으로 예상한다. 
생산 정상화 및 신차 확대를 통한 수익성 개선 기대 
현대차는 글로벌 도매 판매 91.8만대(-11.5% 이하 YoY), 중국 제외 판매량은 90.9
만대(-9.6%)를 기록했다. 7~8월에 발생한 파업 영향으로 약 5.5만대 규모의 생산 차
질이 발생했다. 파업 영향으로 HEV 판매 증가율이 1%에 그치면서, HEV 판매 확대에 
따른 믹스 개선 효과도 시장 기대에 미치지 못했다.  
4분기부터는 3분기 파업 영향으로 지연된 생산량 회복 및 신차 확대가 본격화되면서, 
전분기 대비 매출 및 수익성 개선이 기대된다. 동사는 보급형 전기차 아이오닉3의 튀
르키예 생산을 9월부터 시작했으며, 이를 통해 유럽 시장 판매량은 점차 회복될 전망
이다. 또한, 1) HMGMA를 통한 HEV 미국 현지 생산 확대, 2) 제네시스 HEV 모델 순
차적 출시를 통해 내년부터 북미를 중심으로 믹스 개선이 본격화될 전망이다. 
투자의견은 Buy를 유지하고 목표주가는 520,000원으로 하향 
2027년 영업이익은 11.2조원(+10.2%)를 기록할 것으로 예상된다. 원화 약세 지속을 
반영해 이익 추정치를 하향하며, 이에 따라 목표주가를 하향한다. 다만, 현재 주가는 
12M Fwd P/E 기준 6.1배로 Legacy OEM 평균(7.7배)보다 낮은 수준이다. 1) RMAC 
성과 구체화, 2) RA(로봇 생산법인) 착공 등 Physical AI 사업이 구체화될 경우 밸류에
이션 할증이 가능할 전망이다. 
 Analyst 이병근 
bglee@ls-sec.co.kr 
Buy (유지)

목표주가 (하향) 520,000 원

## Document 283: [012450] 한화에어로스페이스(012450) 방산 납품 계획에 따른 일시적 실적 급감한화에어로스페이스(012450) 방산 납품 계획에 따른 일시적 실_16454052

Source PDF: all report pdf/[012450] 한화에어로스페이스(012450) 방산 납품 계획에 따른 일시적 실적 급감한화에어로스페이스(012450) 방산 납품 계획에 따른 일시적 실_16454052.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

한화에어로스페이스 (012450)

EARNINGS REVIEW Company Analysis | 방위산업 | 2026. 11. 2  
방산 납품 계획에 따른 일시적 실적 급감

방산 납품 계획에 따른 일시적 실적 급감 
지상방산 매출 1조 2,450억원으로 전년비 41%, 영업이익 217억원으로 전년비 
96.2% 급감하며 영업이익률은 1.7% 기록. 실적 급감에 대해 사측은 올해 주요 사업
의 납품 일정이 4분기에 집중됨에 따라 3분기 인도 물량이 적었던 것과 대전 사업장 
생산 중단에 따른 영향 때문이라고 설명. 단, 4분기에는 당초 계획에 따라 주요 사업의 
납품 물량이 집중되어 있어 실적이 개선될 것이고 대전 사업장은 내년 상반기 내에 정
상 가돌 하는 것이 목표라고 밝힘 
수주잔고 증가세 지속으로 성장 지속 가능할 것 
3분기 말 수주잔고 35조원으로 전년비 12.9% 증가. 이를 통한 지상방산 부문 성장 
지속 가능할 것으로 판단. 참고로 사측은 내년 매출 15% 수준 성장 전망 유지. 하지
만 금리 상승에 따른 글로벌 업종 평균 밸류에이션 하락에 따른 방산 부문 적용 멀티
플 하향 조정 그리고 상장 연결회사(한화오션, 한화시스템, 쎄트렉아이)와 상장 비연결
회사(한국항공우주)의 주가 하락 반영 영향으로 목표주가는 하향 조정 
8월 미 육군 차륜형 자주포 사업에서 시제 사업자로 선정. 향후 K9MH는 미 육군의 
작전 운용 개념에 맞춰 맞춤 조정을 거친 후에 특별한 결함 혹은 결격 사유가 없으면 
최종 계약 체결 예정. 동시에 미 육군의 궤도형 자주포 사업에도 참여할 계획. 뿐만 아
니라 스페인 K9, 크로아티아 천무 등으로 신시장 개척 역시 지속되고 있다는 점도 긍
정적

IN THIS ISSUE
Macro Strategy—Guide to El Niño: What It Is, Why It Matters, and What to Do
About It: As crude oil flirts with April’s highs, and the price of diesel sits comfortably
above $6 per gallon, disruption to the global energy complex has emerged as a major
theme of 2026. Add yet another variable to the equation: the current “El Niño” event,

on track to be one of the strongest on record. The upshot: more extreme weather
from droughts to heavy rains, warmer temperatures globally, lower crop production in
commodity-exporting economies, and higher prices across major agricultural products.
Importantly, these pressures won’t be equally distributed and will largely depend on the
magnitude and timing of weather events. Among the most vulnerable will be heavily
import-dependent countries in the Middle East, Africa and parts of Asia, while developed

economies that count food as a smaller share of consumer spending should be better
insulated. For investors, the prospect of a very strong El Niño only further reinforces our
conviction in several structural trends including a return to higher inflation and higher
interest rates, elevated commodity prices amid heightened geopolitical risk and supply
chain shortages, and greater investment in energy infrastructure and supply chain
resilience. In portfolios, we favor commodities as both an inflation hedge and diversifier.

Market View—Another Tale of Market Rotation: October offered another reminder
that headline index returns do not always tell the full story. The S&P 500 ended the month
slightly lower, but the more muted headline return masked meaningful shifts in market
leadership beneath the surface. At the center of the volatility was a sharp unwind in the
Artificial Intelligence (AI) trade, particularly across semiconductors and memory stocks. Yet
pressure on AI market leaders has not translated into a broad retreat from U.S. Equities.

Instead, capital has moved toward previously lagging areas of the market, with Value-
oriented, defensive and rate-sensitive stocks gaining ground. While it remains too early to
determine whether the shift will prove durable, October’s rotation may reflect less a retreat
from the AI theme and more a recalibration of valuations and expected returns as market
leadership broadens. For investors, the speed of the shift reinforces the importance of
diversification as the bull market enters a potentially more mature and differentiated phase.

Thought of the Week—Reorienting Around AI: From Corporate Plans to Construction
to Capital Goods: AI is reshaping the physical economy, and companies are reorienting
capacity and investment plans to meet surging demand for data centers, power and
infrastructure. Industrial firms are adding or reviving capacity, while data center construction
has increased more than 60% over the past year and is outpacing other major construction
categories. At the same time, robust capital goods orders and long lead times for inputs such

as generators, switchboards, chillers and transformers highlight both the strength of demand
and the supply constraints the buildout faces. Taking stock of these signposts, our view
is reinforced that the AI investment cycle remains in its middle innings, creating potential
opportunities for companies supplying the equipment and infrastructure behind the buildout.

### Page 2

2 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Guide to El Niño: What It Is, Why It Matters, and What to Do About It
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
As crude oil flirts with April’s highs, and the price of diesel sits comfortably above $6 per
gallon, disruption to the global energy complex has emerged as a major theme of 2026.
Add yet another variable to the equation: the current “El Niño” event, on track to be one

of the strongest on record. Below we answer frequently asked questions on El Niño,
the potential impact on the global economy and markets and, most importantly, how
investors can position portfolios accordingly.
What is El Niño, and what makes this time different? El Niño is the warm phase
of the “El Niño-Southern Oscillation,” a natural climate pattern occurring every two to
seven years. Simply put, El Niño refers to the warming of sea surface temperatures in

the eastern Pacific, historically leading to drier conditions in some areas (Southeast Asia,
Australia, Central America, southern Africa) and wetter conditions in others (southern
U.S., parts of Latin America) (Exhibit 1).
What makes this episode different? Should forecasts prove correct, this El Niño is on
track to be historic. The National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration’s latest
outlook assigns at least an 83% probability that El Niño’s strength during October-

December will exceed that of any previous episode since 1950. Already, its impact has
been felt via wildfires in Indonesia, the weakest monsoon season in India since 2015, 1
and severe flooding from Paraguay to Peru. The upshot: more extreme weather, hotter
temperatures globally, lower crop production in commodity-exporting economies, and
higher prices across major agricultural products.
Exhibit 1: El Niño's Impact Varies Globally.
Geography Typical rainfall pattern Specific impacts

South Asia Weaker summer monsoon risk Lower crop yields, water shortages, food inflation
Southeast Asia Drier Lower rice and palm oil production, wildfire risk, food inflation
United States Wetter in the south; warmer/drier in the north Southern flood risk; lower northern heating demand
Central America & Northern South America Drier Drought, lower crop yields, water shortages, food inflation, lower hydropower output
Southern Africa Drier Weaker maize harvests, food inflation, lower hydropower output

Western South America Wetter along the Pacific coast Flooding, infrastructure damage, disrupted fisheries
Southern South America Wetter Flooding and crop damage; potential yield gains in some areas
Australia Drier Drought, agricultural losses, elevated wildfire risk, food inflation
Source: National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration, World Meteorological Organization, Food and Agriculture Organization, Australian Bureau of Meteorology, World Bank. Data as of

October 30, 2026.
How will El Niño impact the global economy and markets? Which regions will be
most affected? El Niño’s impact on the global economy and markets is likely to be felt
most via commodity price inflation. Alongside higher energy and fertilizer costs resulting
from war in Europe and the Middle East, El Niño could put further upward pressure on
commodities like wheat, sugar, corn, soy, cocoa and coffee as drought (or extreme rain)
weighs on crop yields and production. Latest estimates from the United Nations Food

and Agriculture Organization (FAO) suggest global food prices were up 5.8% year-over-
year (YoY) in September (Exhibit 2). Livestock is also likely to be challenged by higher
temperatures globally, while flooding has already threatened copper production in
Chile. Meanwhile, with hydropower accounting for more than half of the average annual
electricity generation in Brazil, Colombia, Peru and other Latin American countries, 2
below-average rainfall in affected areas has huge implications for electricity generation.

As an example, Colombia’s natural gas demand nearly doubled during the 2023-2024 El
Niño event.2 Another key watchpoint will be whether drier conditions reduce water levels
1 India Meteorological Department, Bloomberg. Refers to rainfall between June and September as of September 30, 2026.
2 International Energy Agency, as of October 7, 2026
Investment Implications
We expect El Niño to place upward
pressure on agricultural goods
prices globally. We continue to

see commodities as a strategic
diversifier and hedge against inflation
in portfolios. The U.S. remains our
preferred region with companies in
Industrials and Materials as potential
beneficiaries. Meanwhile, economic
impacts to the emerging world will vary
by country but the broader Emerging
Market (EM) equity market could be
less exposed given its significant tilt
toward Technology.

### Page 3

3 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
in the Panama Canal and other key shipping routes, particularly in light of the Strait of
Hormuz closure.
These pressures won’t be equally distributed and will heavily depend on this El Niño’s
severity. Among the more vulnerable to higher agricultural prices will be heavily import-
dependent countries in the Middle East, Africa, and parts of Asia. So too will economies
that count food as a larger share of consumer spending such as the Philippines (36%),

Indonesia (33%), Egypt (33%), India (28%), and Vietnam (26%) (Exhibit 3). Developed
economies like the U.S. (6%), Canada (10%), and Germany (11%) stand to be better
insulated from a food-related shock to headline inflation.
Within the U.S., some impacts could be positive with a potentially milder winter reducing
heating costs and wetter conditions in the South improving agricultural conditions. A recent
paper from the Federal Reserve Bank of San Francisco found that, in addition to putting

upward pressure on personal consumption expenditures (PCE) inflation, strong El Niño
episodes have been historically associated with a temporary boost to employment in the
following year via better construction conditions in the North and weather-event recovery
efforts.3 In California and parts of the Southwest, El Niño will be yet another stress test for
property insurers as flooding and landslide risks increase.
For more vulnerable developing economies, meanwhile, higher food and energy costs could

present a policy dilemma. On one hand, governments in vulnerable areas may move to
introduce fiscal relief to support households and boost growth. History points to plenty of
examples of this, such as Indonesia’s cash assistance in late 2023 or Thailand’s $1.8 billion
in loans to farmers in 2015. On the other hand, elevated inflation expectations and, in
some cases, weaker currencies could limit EM central banks’ room to ease monetary policy,
particularly with several major developed central banks aligned in hiking interest rates. Also

complicating matters: Global sovereign debt surpassed $110 trillion in Q2 2026, leaving
less fiscal space to absorb incoming shocks. 4
How can investors position portfolios accordingly? In many ways, the prospect of a
very strong El Niño only reinforces structural trends we’ve identified as defining the coming
years: a return to higher inflation and higher interest rates; elevated commodity prices
amid heightened geopolitical risk and resource/supply shortages; and greater investment in

energy infrastructure and supply chain resilience.
Considering these themes, we continue to like commodities not just as a hedge against
higher inflation but also as a diversification tool in portfolios given their negative correlation
with Equities and bonds. That’s not to mention secular forces placing upward pressure on
commodity prices such as the resource-intensive AI buildout and broader protectionism. To
wit, the Bloomberg Commodity Index, which spans oil, gold, various metals and agricultural

goods has gained nearly 30% this year, handily outperforming the S&P 500 (+1%) and the
Bloomberg U.S. Aggregate Bond Index (-1%).
Within Equities, the U.S. is still our preferred region. While upside risk to inflation remains
a risk to watch going into 2027, food is a relatively small share of the consumer basket in
the U.S., and neither strong services spending nor capital investment should be materially
affected by El Niño. Companies within the Industrials and Materials sectors focused on

precision agriculture, water management and crop protection may be positioned to benefit.
Import-dependent EM economies remain particularly exposed, though impacts will vary
widely depending on the magnitude and timing of weather events. It’s worth noting too that
EM Equities remain heavily concentrated in East Asian markets with substantial exposure to
technology, leaving the broader index arguably less exposed to the economic ramifications
of El Niño than in past episodes. Indeed, even after recent weakness in semiconductor

names, Taiwan, Korea, and China together account for a near-70% of the broader MSCI
Emerging Markets Index. 5 We maintain a slight overweight to the region in portfolios given
earnings strength but favor an active approach.
3 Federal Reserve Bank of San Francisco, October 13, 2026.
4 Institute of International Finance, as of September 23, 2026.
5 FactSet, as of October 27, 2026.

4 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Another Tale of Market Rotation
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
October offered another reminder that headline index returns do not always tell the
full story. The S&P 500 ended the month slightly lower, down almost 1%, but the more
muted headline return masked meaningful shifts in market leadership beneath the
surface. At the center of the volatility was a sharp unwind in the AI trade, particularly

across semiconductors and memory stocks, with the Philadelphia Semiconductor Index
experiencing a 19% intramonth drawdown. The dominant concerns in recent months
have centered on the sustainability of AI infrastructure spending and whether the scale
of investment will ultimately generate sufficient returns. Those only intensified amid a
sharp rise in bond yields after the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield spiked above 5.3% as of
October 5 for the first time since 2002, raising scrutiny over AI financing conditions and

whether higher borrowing costs could increase hurdle rates for continued investment
and slow the pace of capital spending.
Yet the pressure on AI market leaders has not translated into a broad retreat from U.S.
Equities. Instead, capital has moved toward previously lagging areas of the market,
with Value-oriented, defensive and rate-sensitive stocks gaining ground. Value defeated
Growth in October, with the Russell 1000 Value Index outperforming its Growth

counterpart by 10 percentage points, an outcome seen in only about 1% of months since
1979. The move extends Value's outperformance for the year, with the index up 23%
year-to-date compared with down almost 1% for Growth. Participation also improved.
The equal-weighted S&P 500 outperformed its market-cap-weighted counterpart by its
widest monthly gap since 2009 (Exhibit 4A), while the share of S&P 500 constituents
trading above their 200-day moving average rebounded to 62% from just 43% at the

end of September, and 76% of stocks outperformed the index for the month.
Exhibit 4: Market Leadership Has Been Shifting Beneath the Surface.
A) October Saw a Sharp Broadening in Market Leadership. B) Value Has Quietly Led Growth in 2026.

not possible to invest directly in an index.

The latest rotation is not the first attempt at a change in market leadership this year.
The more important question is what that shift represents: a fundamental reassessment
of the AI trade or another step toward broader, more mature market leadership?
Recent Q3 Technology earnings results have sharpened that debate. Investors are
increasingly looking beyond the scale of AI spending to companies’ ability to translate
that investment into durable growth and attractive returns. The result may be greater

dispersion not only between Growth and Value, but also among AI leaders as investors
become more discerning about which companies can justify the scale of their
investments.
Investment Implications
We continue to look for potential
missing opportunities and favor
diversification across and within asset
classes in a multi-asset portfolio.
In particular, we see potential
opportunities to broaden exposure
beyond AI leaders toward areas
where earnings, capital spending and

productivity trends are improving,
including Value, Small- and Mid-caps,
and select non-U.S. markets, particularly
EMs and Japan. As market leadership
evolves, maintaining diversified
exposures and periodically rebalancing
portfolios can help investors participate
in a broader set of possible return
drivers without becoming overly
dependent on any single theme.

### Page 5

5 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
But greater selectivity does not necessarily signal the end of the AI investment cycle.
YoY growth rates may become harder to maintain after two years of rapid acceleration
as the comparison base rises, but absolute levels of AI capital spending remain elevated.
What matters more is whether spending translates into strong productivity and earnings
growth ahead and whether AI demand continues to support investment. October’s

volatility may therefore reflect less a retreat from the AI theme and more a recalibration
of valuations and expected returns, as other areas of the market begin to contribute
more meaningfully to market performance.
Several developments ahead will help determine whether that rotation has staying
power. One important test is whether broader participation can persist if pressure on AI
stocks continues. Continued strength outside the AI trade would support the case that

investors are reallocating capital rather than broadly reducing risk. The path of monetary
policy will also matter as investors assess where borrowing costs may be headed. The
Federal Reserve’s not-so-hawkish hold in October offered some reassurance, but the
path ahead remains dependent on the evolving balance between inflation and growth.
Any signs of resolution of geopolitical tensions remain an important swing factor
particularly as recent flashpoints in the Middle East have pressured energy prices higher

again. Results of the midterm elections should also provide greater visibility into the
policy landscape for the next few years. While the market does not need every clearing
event to resolve favorably, greater clarity around these uncertainties could help narrow
the range of potential outcomes facing investors.
For investors, October’s sudden change in leadership reinforces the importance of
diversification. Leadership can shift quickly, leaving portfolios that become overly

dependent on a single theme, sector or style particularly vulnerable when it does. We
remain constructive on U.S. Equities and believe that the market may be transitioning
more toward a mid-cycle phase in which returns become broader but also more
measured compared to the sharp acceleration earlier in the cycle. That argues not for
abandoning the market’s long-term winners, but for complementing them with exposure
to additional sources of returns across style, size, sector and region.

### Page 6

6 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Reorienting around AI: From Corporate Plans to Construction to Capital Goods
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
For all the questions (and recent market weakness) hanging over the AI trade, from
monetization and cash burn to financing risk and overcapacity, a measurable aspect is
the buildout itself. Data center construction is lifting construction spending and capital

goods orders, while companies are adding and reviving capacity to meet AI-related
demand. Still, planning delays, access to power, labor scarcity, local politics and the cost
of capital remain meaningful constraints on the buildout’s pace or profitability.
Corporate Plans. Take Caterpillar as one example that has resumed production of a
10-megawatt natural gas turbine discontinued back in 2022. A 175-year old glassmaker,
Corning, is leaning into optical connectivity as AI clusters require faster connections of

optical fiber cabling between servers. Caterpillar and Corning are just two companies
among others pivoting to supply data centers.
Construction to Capital Goods. The AI reorientation is evident in construction
spending as well, where data center construction has outpaced every other major
residential and nonresidential category. Over the last year AI-related construction on data
centers has surged more than 60% while spending on residential, lodging, commercial

or office has declined notably (Exhibit 5A). Data center construction is running at an $85
billion (B) annualized rate this year, versus roughly $49B a year earlier, and now accounts
for 11% of private nonresidential construction, up from just 2% in 2022 according to
the Census Bureau. Similarly, demand for artificial intelligence business equipment and
transportation equipment drove an increase in durable goods orders in September on the
back of new orders for U.S.-manufactured capital goods, suggesting business spending

on equipment remained robust in Q3. Another factor, see Exhibit 5B, where critical
components such as generators, switchboards, chillers and transformers continue to
face long lead times that affect delivery timelines and ultimate profitability for new data
center development.
These signposts reinforce our view that we are only in the middle innings of the AI
investment cycle. From an investment perspective, huge demand for data centers, power
systems, networking infrastructure, specialized chips and other equipment continues

to strain supply, creating both bottlenecks and potential opportunities for companies
powering the buildout.

with demand, supply bottlenecks may

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500/Market-cap Weighted Index is a market-capitalization-weighted index that is widely regarded as the best single gauge of large-cap U.S. equities. The index includes 500 leading

companies and covers approximately 80% of available market capitalization.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index includes the same constituents as the capitalization weighted S&P 500, but each company in the S&P 500 EWI is allocated a fixed weight - or 0.2% of the
index total at each quarterly rebalance.
Bloomberg U.S. Aggregate Bond Index is a broad-based, market-capitalization-weighted benchmark that measures the performance of the U.S. investment-grade, U.S. dollar-denominated,

fixed-rate taxable bond market
Bloomberg Commodity Index is a broadly diversified financial benchmark that tracks the price movements of physical commodities through futures contracts.
MSCI Emerging Market Index captures large and mid cap representation across 24 Emerging Markets (EM) countries.
United Nations Food and Agriculture Food Price Index is a measure of the monthly change in international prices of a basket of food commodities.
Philadelphia Semiconductor Index is a market-cap-weighted index focusing on 30 leading semiconductor firms, offering insights into the tech market.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

## Document 357: Cliff Notes_ the source and persistence of inflation (2026-11-10)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ the source and persistence of inflation (2026-11-10).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-10
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: the source and persistence of inflation
By Ryan Wells, Illiana Jain & Elliot Clarke
11:16 October 30 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia this week, all eyes were on the September CPI. Headline inflation rose 0.8% (4.3%yr) in
the month, largely owing to a 9.4% jump in fuel prices and a seasonal surge in domestic and
international holiday travel & accommodation. The underlying inflation pulse also proved hotter than

anticipated for Q3 overall, up 1.0% (3.7%yr).

### Page 2

While some parts of the basket are seeing disinflation – housing in particular looks to be cooling faster
than expected as builders lure sales with discounts – other components are still showing persistence.
Highlighting this is market services ex volatiles, which the RBA watches closely as an indicator of price
pressures related to domestic capacity. In Q3, this metric rose 1.2% (3.2%yr), and the monthly detail
points to momentum holding up – in line with our expectations for another chunky read in Q4.

With inflation running well above the RBA’s August forecasts, and some of the energy risks flagged
then materialising, we continue to expect the Monetary Policy Board will deliver a 25bp rate hike to
4.85% in November. Beyond November, another rate hike in February is a risk, but is not currently part
of our base case.
Across the Tasman, the RBNZ this week surprised market expectations for a 25bp rate hike, deciding
instead to wait for further information before taking the next step with policy. The decision was made

4:3, with the Governor in the ‘on hold’ camp. A 25bp hike in December to 3.00% and further tightening
in 2027 (Westpac NZ forecasts a peak of 4.00% in September) seems probable; however, each
decision will depend on fuel prices, inflation expectations and evidence of second-round pricing
pressures, along with the evolution of global economic risks.
In the US, the FOMC also held rates steady at its October meeting. Based on prior comments by key
members, the decision was not a surprise, and neither were Governor Cook and Cleveland Fed

President Hammack’s dissents in favour of a 25bp hike. The Committee remains positive on economic
activity, characterising it in the statement as “expanding at a strong pace” (an upgrade from “solid” in
September). Chair Warsh also subsequently emphasised the breadth of economic growth outside of
tech-related investment (evinced by Q3 GDP the day after; see below).
The Committee refrained from spotlighting key price risks in the statement. However, inflation “remains

elevated” and the FOMC are determined to achieve a “timely” and sustainable return of annual inflation
to 2.0%yr after 6 years above target. This guidance and the “accumulation of more information will help
the Committee determine the policy stance” suggests another 25bp hike is likely in December.
Thereafter, the FOMC will remain attentive to any broadening in inflation and related risks through
2027; although, in the absence of another shock, we expect them to remain on hold.

The Q3 GDP report certainly supported Chair Warsh’s view on the breadth of growth, the 3.4%
annualised gain for GDP benefitting from a 3.8% annualised increase in household consumption,
12.7% growth in non-residential investment as well as a robust increase in government spending.
Notably though, the contribution from business investment was effectively offset by net exports’ drag
given most of the componentry related to AI infrastructure investment is imported. This is not to say that

US firms won’t benefit from the investment once complete, but simply that much of the initial return
goes to the foreign businesses who provide the necessary capital equipment.
On consumption, the monthly PCE data implies downside risks remain for the consumer, the increase
in nominal spending seen in the month requiring dissaving and, on a real basis, consumption flat in the
month following a 0.5% gain in August. Cost-of-living pressures and uncertainty over the outlook is

### Page 3

Related articles
expected to see household consumption growth average around trend over the coming year, albeit with
considerable volatility quarter to quarter. This should see PCE inflation slow back towards its 2.0%yr
medium-term target as the FOMC desires, from 3.6%yr and 3.1%yr for the headline and core
measures respectively as of September.
Across the Atlantic, the ECB also left its key policy rates unchanged in October. Of note from the
meeting communications, energy prices continue to pose upside risks for inflation, but also downside

risks for growth. "While the full effects of the energy shock are still unfolding, underlying inflation has
remained broadly stable so far and wages do not show a material response". "Financing conditions
have [also] tightened", "which is likely to weigh on inflation and growth". In the press conference,
President Lagarde noted that, between the October and December meetings, a significant amount of
new economic information will be released, allowing forecasts and risks assessments to be tested.

Based on the October meeting communications, we continue to expect a rate hike at the December
meeting but believe this will be prove to be the last hike of the cycle. Note though, the spectrum of risks
Europe faces is broad and skewed towards inflation in the near term, hence the Council’s guidance will
remain hawkish. President Lagarde also announced today that she will step down as ECB President in
February ahead of the scheduled conclusion of her term next October.
